```nocturne/examples/drl_collision_avoidance/evaluate.py
     1	import argparse
     2	import os
     3	import sys
     4	from typing import Optional
     5	
     6	import numpy as np
     7	import torch
     8	
     9	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))
    10	
    11	from cfgs.config import set_display_window
    12	from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
    13	from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config
    14	
    15	
    16	def _checkpoint_obs_dim(checkpoint_path: str) -> Optional[int]:
    17	    checkpoint = torch.load(checkpoint_path, map_location='cpu', weights_only=False)
    18	    if not isinstance(checkpoint, dict):
    19	        return None
    20	    if checkpoint.get('obs_dim') is not None:
    21	        return int(checkpoint['obs_dim'])
    22	
    23	    state_dict = checkpoint.get('online_net') or checkpoint.get('model')
    24	    hidden_layers = checkpoint.get('hidden_layers')
    25	    grid_size = checkpoint.get('grid_size')
    26	    if not state_dict or not hidden_layers or grid_size is None:
    27	        return None
    28	
    29	    encoder_dim = int(hidden_layers[1])
    30	    head_weight_names = {
    31	        'advantage_head.initial_layer.0.weight',
    32	        'advantage_head.initial_layer.0.weight_mu',
    33	        'head.initial_layer.0.weight',
    34	        'head.initial_layer.0.weight_mu',
    35	    }
    36	    for key, value in state_dict.items():
    37	        normalized_key = key
    38	        changed = True
    39	        while changed:
    40	            changed = False
    41	            for prefix in ('module.', '_orig_mod.'):
    42	                if normalized_key.startswith(prefix):
    43	                    normalized_key = normalized_key[len(prefix):]
    44	                    changed = True
    45	        if normalized_key in head_weight_names:
    46	            head_input_dim = int(value.shape[1])
    47	            return int(grid_size) + head_input_dim - encoder_dim
    48	    return None
    49	
    50	
    51	def _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, checkpoint_path, use_occlusion):
    52	    grid_cfg = cfg['occupancy_grid']
    53	    if use_occlusion is not None:
    54	        grid_cfg['use_occlusion'] = bool(use_occlusion)
    55	        return
    56	
    57	    ckpt_obs_dim = _checkpoint_obs_dim(checkpoint_path)
    58	    if ckpt_obs_dim is None:
    59	        return
    60	

```


## Assistant

    Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the current state. Let me summarize what exists and what is missing, then provide a prioritized roadmap.

I have thoroughly inspected the `nocturne` codebase. Based on your research goals, here is a clear audit of what is already implemented versus what you still need to build.

### What Exists ✅

| Feature | Location | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **DDQN Training** | `examples/drl_collision_avoidance/train.py` | Actively running (your logs). |
| **Occlusion Features** | `collision_avoidance_env.py` | `use_occlusion` toggle adds 4 occlusion features (occ_frac, nearest_blocker, density, pressure). |
| **Occlusion Evaluation** | `scripts/interpretability/occlusion_behavior.py` | Plots speed vs. occlusion fraction. |
| **Density Analysis** | `scripts/density_analysis.py` | Groups results by vehicle count bins (1-4, 5-9, 10-14, 15+). |
| **ADE / FDE** | `evaluate.py` | Computes Average Displacement Error and Final Displacement Error. |
| **Jerk & Smoothness** | `evaluate.py` | Computes jerk (m/s³) and steering rate smoothness. |
| **Statistical Testing** | `scripts/compute_statistics.py` | Bootstrap CI, mean ± std, Mann-Whitney U-test. |
| **Safety Constraints** | `algos/ppo/r_mappo/` | R-MAPPO with Lagrangian cost critic already exists. |
| **Behavioral Cloning** | `examples/drl_collision_avoidance/train_bc.py` | Collects expert trajectories and trains a classifier. |
| **Visualization** | `examples/drl_collision_avoidance/visualize.py` | Renders MP4 videos of trained agents. |
| **Continuous Control (CRL)** | `crl_modules/actor.py` | SAC-style actor exists for continuous actions. |

### What is Missing / Needs Work ❌

#### 1. **Robust Occlusion Avoidance Under Partial Observability**
- **The Gap:** You have `use_occlusion=True` and feature extraction, but there is no systematic evaluation campaign that tests the same checkpoint against **varying sensor ranges** or **occlusion levels**. You cannot answer "How does my agent degrade when visibility is reduced by X%?"
- **Action Required:** Create a script that evaluates a single checkpoint under a grid of `occlusion_view_angle`, `view_dist`, and `max_visible_objects` values. Save the results to compare "robustness curves."

#### 2. **Safety-Critical / Risk-Aware RL**
- **The Gap:** The current DDQN reward function penalizes collisions (-100) but does not explicitly model **risk** or **worst-case outcomes**.
- **Action Required:** You need to define "risk metrics" (e.g., minimum Time-To-Collision, probability of collision in the next 2 seconds) and either:
  - Modify the reward to penalize high-risk states even if no collision occurs.
  - Or implement a separate Value-at-Risk (VaR) / Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR) objective over the buffer.

#### 3. **Penalty / Goal Rate / Collision Rate Trade-off**
- **The Gap:** `evaluate.py` computes these, but there is no automated sweep to generate a **Pareto front** of safety vs. performance.
- **Action Required:** Use the existing `scripts/pareto_sweep.sh` (or create a Python equivalent) to sweep penalty weights (collision, step, offroad) and plot the resulting Goal Rate vs. Collision Rate curve.

#### 4. **Collision vs. Density**
- **The Gap:** `density_analysis.py` gives you a table per method, but you cannot yet plot "Collision Rate vs. Number of Agents" as a continuous curve with error bars.
- **Action Required:** Modify `evaluate.py` to log `num_vehicles` for every episode, then post-process with a script that computes collision rate per vehicle count with 95% CIs.

#### 5. **Mean ± Std + Confidence / Statistical Tests**
- **The Gap:** `compute_statistics.py` is already good for this, but it is **not automated** into your evaluation pipeline. You are likely running a single eval per checkpoint.
- **Action Required:** Run evaluation with **N seeds** (at least 5, ideally 10) for every configuration. Use `compute_statistics.py` to produce the final paper table.

#### 6. **Interpretability**
- **The Gap:** Only one script (`occlusion_behavior.py`) exists. There is no attention visualization, no saliency maps, and no action-preference analysis.
- **Action Required:** Add saliency/attention hooks to the Q-Network to visualize which grid cells influence action selection.

#### 7. **Continuous vs. Discrete Control**
- **The Gap:** You have `discretize_actions: False` in config, but the `train.py` pipeline is heavily built around discrete action tables. `crl_modules/actor.py` exists but is not integrated into the main `train.py` loop.
- **Action Required:** To compare discrete vs. continuous, you need a unified training script that can switch between DDQN and CRL/SAC without manual code changes.

#### 8. **ADE / SDE / Jerk & Smoothness**
- **The Gap:** ADE and jerk are logged per episode, but there is no summary metric for **Standard Displacement Error (SDE)** or **trajectory smoothness** across the entire test set with variance.
- **Action Required:** Extend `evaluate.py` to compute SDE and a holistic "comfort score" combining jerk, acceleration, and steering rate.

---

### What You Need To Do Right Now

Because you are actively training on EC2, the most actionable immediate steps are:

1. **Run Evaluation Campaigns in Parallel:** Spin up multiple EC2 jobs or use `tmux` on your current instance to evaluate your checkpoints under different `occlusion_view_angle` values.
2. **Track Losses:** Your log format does not show `td_loss` or `q`. You need to verify that `learner.latest_loss` is actually being populated and printed. If `q=0` in your logs, that is a bug or a placeholder.
3. **Add Continuous Eval Logging:** Every 10k episodes (or better, every fixed number of env steps), run a zero-epsilon eval and log `eval_goal_rate`, `eval_collision_rate`, and `eval_avg_reward`. This is the only way to know if your agent is actually learning.
4. **Save TensorBoard Logs:** The code uses `SummaryWriter`, but ensure you are pulling those logs off EC2 for local inspection.
5. **Run Multi-Seed Training:** If you are trusting a single seed, your results are not statistically sound. Launch at least 3 independent training runs.

### Questions for You

1. Are you running the **DDQN** (`train.py`) or the **CRL** (`train_crl.py`) pipeline?
2. What is the value of `q=0` in your training log? Is that `latest_loss`, `queue_size`, or something else?
3. Do you have a specific deadline for this paper submission? (This affects whether I prioritize analysis code over new algorithm development.)

```nocturne/examples/drl_collision_avoidance/train.py
   710	        writer.add_scalar('learner/last_weight_sync_step', learner.last_weight_sync_step, episodes_completed)
   711	
   712	
   713	def _maybe_print_episode_log(
   714	    episodes_completed,
   715	    log_interval,
   716	    reward_history,
   717	    start_time,
   718	    global_step,
   719	    learner,
   720	    agent,
   721	    info,
   722	):
   723	    if episodes_completed % log_interval != 0:
   724	        return
   725	
   726	    avg_rew = np.mean(reward_history[-log_interval:])
   727	    elapsed = max(time.time() - start_time, 1e-3)
   728	    fps = int(global_step / elapsed)
   729	    buf_sz = learner.buffer_size if learner else len(agent.replay_buffer)
   730	    train_steps = learner.train_steps if learner else agent.train_steps
   731	    queue_fragment = ''
   732	    if learner is not None:
   733	        queue_fragment = (

   735	            f' | lag={learner.train_step_lag:4d}'
   736	            f' | dropped={learner.dropped_batches:4d}'
   737	        )
   738	    print(
   739	        f'Episode {episodes_completed:5d} | '
   740	        f'avg_reward={avg_rew:8.2f} | '
   741	        f'eps={agent.epsilon:.3f} | '
   742	        f'goal_dist={info.get("goal_dist", float("inf")):.1f} | '
   743	        f'buf={buf_sz:6d} | '
   744	        f'env_steps={global_step} | '
   745	        f'train_steps={train_steps}'
   746	        f'{queue_fragment}'
   747	        f' | fps={fps}'
   748	    )
   749	
   750
```

```nocturne/examples/drl_collision_avoidance/scenario_utils.py
     1	import hashlib
     2	import os
     3	import subprocess
     4	from typing import Any, Dict, Optional
     5	
     6	from omegaconf import OmegaConf
     7	
     8	from cfgs.config import PROCESSED_TRAIN_NO_TL, PROCESSED_VALID_NO_TL
     9	
    10	_SUPPORTED_SPLITS = ('train', 'valid')
    11	_CONFIG_PATH = os.path.join(
    12	    os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..', 'cfgs', 'drl_collision_avoidance', 'config.yaml'
    13	)
    14	
    15	# Per-process cache: S3 URI → local path. Prevents redundant syncs when
    16	# multiple envs in the same worker process share an S3 scenario_path.
    17	_s3_sync_cache: Dict[str, str] = {}
    18	
    19	
    20	def _default_path_for_split(split: str) -> str:
    21	    if split == 'train':
    22	        return os.environ.get('PROCESSED_TRAIN_NO_TL', PROCESSED_TRAIN_NO_TL)
    23	    if split == 'valid':
    24	        return os.environ.get('PROCESSED_VALID_NO_TL', PROCESSED_VALID_NO_TL)
    25	    raise ValueError(
    26	        f'Unsupported scenario_split={split!r}. '
    27	        f'Expected one of {_SUPPORTED_SPLITS}.'
    28	    )
    29	
    30	
    31	def _is_s3_path(path: str) -> bool:
    32	    return path.startswith('s3://')
    33	
    34	
    35	def _sync_s3_to_local(s3_uri: str) -> str:
    36	    """Sync an S3 prefix to a local temp directory and return the local path.
    37	
    38	    Safe to call concurrently from multiple Ray actors — aws s3 sync is
    39	    idempotent and each URI maps to a unique cache directory via SHA-256 hash.
    40	    Each actor process syncs at most once per URI thanks to _s3_sync_cache.
    41	    """
    42	    if s3_uri in _s3_sync_cache:
    43	        return _s3_sync_cache[s3_uri]
    44	    cache_key = hashlib.sha256(s3_uri.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    45	    local_dir = os.path.join('/tmp', 'nocturne_s3_cache', cache_key)
    46	    os.makedirs(local_dir, exist_ok=True)
    47	    try:
    48	        subprocess.run(
    49	            ['aws', 's3', 'sync', s3_uri.rstrip('/') + '/', local_dir + '/', '--no-progress'],
    50	            check=True,
    51	            capture_output=True,
    52	            text=True,
    53	        )
    54	    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as exc:
    55	        stderr = exc.stderr.strip() if exc.stderr else ''
    56	        stdout = exc.stdout.strip() if exc.stdout else ''
    57	        details = stderr or stdout or 'No subprocess output captured.'
    58	        raise RuntimeError(
    59	            f'Failed to sync S3 scenario path {s3_uri!r} to local cache {local_dir!r}: '
    60	            f'{details}'
    61	        ) from exc
    62	    _s3_sync_cache[s3_uri] = local_dir
    63	    return local_dir
    64	
    65	
    66	def resolve_scenario_path(
    67	    scenario_path: Optional[str],
    68	    scenario_split: str = 'train',
    69	) -> str:
    70	    split = str(scenario_split).lower()
    71	    resolved_path = scenario_path or _default_path_for_split(split)
    72	
    73	    # S3 URIs are synced to local per-worker at env creation time (in _make_env_fn).
    74	    # Pass them through here without filesystem checks.
    75	    if _is_s3_path(resolved_path):
    76	        return resolved_path
    77	
    78	    resolved_path = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(str(resolved_path)))
    79	
    80	    if not os.path.isdir(resolved_path):
    81	        raise FileNotFoundError(
    82	            f'Scenario directory does not exist: {resolved_path}. '
    83	            f'Pass --scenario_path explicitly or set scenario_split to one of '
    84	            f'{_SUPPORTED_SPLITS}.'
    85	        )
    86	
    87	    valid_files_path = os.path.join(resolved_path, 'valid_files.json')
    88	    if not os.path.isfile(valid_files_path):
    89	        raise FileNotFoundError(
    90	            f'Missing valid_files.json in scenario directory: {resolved_path}. '
    91	            'Nocturne BaseEnv requires this file.'
    92	        )
    93	
    94	    return resolved_path
    95	
    96	
    97	def apply_scenario_path_defaults(
    98	    cfg: Dict[str, Any],
    99	    default_split: str = 'train',
   100	) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   101	    split = str(cfg.get('scenario_split', default_split)).lower()
   102	    cfg['scenario_split'] = split
   103	    cfg['scenario_path'] = resolve_scenario_path(cfg.get('scenario_path'), split)
   104	    return cfg
   105	
   106	
   107	def load_config(
   108	    scenario_path: Optional[str] = None,
   109	    scenario_split: str = 'valid',
   110	    num_files: int = -1,
   111	    **overrides,
   112	) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   113	    cfg = OmegaConf.load(_CONFIG_PATH)
   114	    cfg_dict = OmegaConf.to_container(cfg, resolve=True)
   115	
   116	    if scenario_split:
   117	        cfg_dict['scenario_split'] = scenario_split
   118	    if scenario_path:
   119	        cfg_dict['scenario_path'] = scenario_path
   120	    if num_files != -1:
   121	        cfg_dict['num_files'] = num_files
   122	
   123	    for key, value in overrides.items():
   124	        cfg_dict[key] = value
   125	
   126	    return apply_scenario_path_defaults(cfg_dict, default_split=scenario_split)
   127	

```

```python
    _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, checkpoint_path, use_occlusion)

```

Let me write the script. I'll assume `build_cpu_agent` and `load_config` are available.

For the `build_cpu_agent`, the `cfg` dict must contain `occupancy_grid` and `drl` keys.

Let me think about the occlusion features. If `use_occlusion` is true, the observation dimension increases by 4. The `agent` (DDQNAgent) was trained with a specific `obs_dim`. If we evaluate with `use_occlusion=True` but the agent was trained without it, the `agent.load()` will likely fail because the network dimensions don't match.

_This is a critical point for the robustness sweep._ When testing "occlusion" vs "no occlusion", we must use a checkpoint that was trained with the corresponding observation dimension.

There are two common research approaches:
A) Train *one* agent with occlusion features enabled, then evaluate it as you degrade the *sensor model* (reduce view_dist, reduce view_angle) while still feeding the occlusion features into the network. The network input size stays constant, but the feature values degrade.
B) Train two agents: one with occlusion features, one without. Then compare them.

Approach A is much more practical for a robustness sweep. You train with `use_occlusion=True` and `occlusion_view_angle=pi`. Then for evaluation, you keep `use_occlusion=True` but reduce `view_dist` and `occlusion_view_angle`. The network still gets the 4 occlusion features, but they are computed with a narrower/degraded sensor model.

Wait, looking at `collision_avoidance_env.py`:
```python
visible_objects = self.base_env.scenario.visible_state(
    ego_veh, view_dist=view_dist, view_angle=self.occlusion_view_angle, ...
)
```
So `view_dist` and `view_angle` are passed to the C++ `visible_state`. If we train with `use_occlusion=True` and `occlusion_view_angle=pi`, the `visible_state` sees a full circle. If we evaluate with `occlusion_view_angle=pi/2`, the agent sees fewer objects.

This is perfect for a robustness sweep. The network structure doesn't change. The observation content changes.

But what if the user trained with `use_occlusion=False` (the default)? Then the network expects `obs_dim = grid_size + 9`. If we try to evaluate with `use_occlusion=True`, the `CollisionAvoidanceEnv` initializes with `obs_dim = grid_size + 13`, and then `build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)` creates an agent with `obs_dim=grid_size+13`. But the checkpoint has weights for `obs_dim=grid_size+9`. This will throw a size mismatch error.

So the sweep should probably warn or abort if the checkpoint was not trained with occlusion.

Let me check `_apply_checkpoint_observation_settings` again. It sets `use_occlusion` based on the checkpoint's `obs_dim`. My sweep script should do the same, or better, let the user specify it, and warn if it conflicts.

For simplicity, I'll make the sweep script require `use_occlusion` to match the checkpoint. If the user wants to sweep occlusion levels, they should use a checkpoint trained *with* occlusion.

Actually, the most useful thing for the user's research is to show:
1. Agent trained with full observability, evaluated under degraded observability (just change `view_dist`, `view_angle` in the env, *without* adding occlusion features to the observation). This tests raw robustness.
2. Agent trained with occlusion features, evaluated under degraded observability (change `view_dist`, `view_angle`, but keep `use_occlusion=True` so the agent gets the features).

Case 1 is what they probably have now (trained with `use_occlusion=False`, default). They can just change `subscriber.view_dist` in the config for evaluation.

Case 2 requires a separate training run with `use_occlusion=True`.

Given the current state (they have a training run ongoing), let's assume they want to evaluate Case 1 first (degrade sensor inputs without changing the observation space). This is the simplest robustness test.

For the sweep script, I'll implement:
- Load checkpoint, infer `use_occlusion` from checkpoint dimension.
- Loop over `view_dist` values: [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
- Loop over `occlusion_view_angle` values: [3.14, 1.57, 0.78, 0.39] (in radians, π, π/2, π/4, π/8)
- For each config, create the env with modified subscriber settings, run 100 eval episodes.
- Save JSON per config.
- Print a summary table of Goal Rate, Collision Rate, Avg Reward for each sensor config.

This is clean and doesn't require re-training.

For the `run_evaluation_suite.py`, I'll make it more focused: just run a standard eval, maybe with a few different `num_files` or scenario splits, and generate a video. It's basically a thin wrapper around `evaluate.py` that adds `--output_json` and `--render` convenience.

**Script 1: `occlusion_robustness_sweep.py`**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Occlusion Robustness Sweep.

Evaluate a trained checkpoint across varying sensor capabilities
(view distance, view angle) to produce robustness curves for
partially observable driving.
"""

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
from typing import List, Optional, Tuple

import numpy as np
import torch

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.evaluate import _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings, _checkpoint_obs_dim
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config


def run_eval_for_config(checkpoint_path: str, cfg: dict, num_episodes: int = 100) -> dict:
    """Run a single evaluation with a fully prepared config dict."""
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint_path)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0

    results = {
        'rewards': [], 'lengths': [],
        'collisions': 0, 'goals': 0, 'timeouts': 0,
        'collided_list': [], 'goal_list': [], 'timeout_list': [],
        'ttz_vehicles': [], 'ttz_pedestrians': [],
    }

    for ep in range(1, num_episodes + 1):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward = 0.0
        collided = False
        goal = False

        for t in range(cfg['drl']['max_episode_steps']):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            done = terminated or truncated
            total_reward += reward
            collided = collided or info.get('collided', False)
            goal = goal or info.get('goal_achieved', False)
            ttz_vehicle = info.get('ttz_vehicle', 999.0)
            ttz_pedestrian = info.get('ttz_pedestrian', 999.0)
            results['ttz_vehicles'].append(ttz_vehicle)
            results['ttz_pedestrians'].append(ttz_pedestrian)
            if done:
                break

        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results['rewards'].append(total_reward)
        results['lengths'].append(t + 1)
        results['collisions'] += int(collided)
        results['goals'] += int(goal)
        results['timeouts'] += int(timeout)
        results['collided_list'].append(collided)
        results['goal_list'].append(goal)
        results['timeout_list'].append(timeout)

    summary = {
        'num_episodes': num_episodes,
        'avg_reward': float(np.mean(results['rewards'])),
        'std_reward': float(np.std(results['rewards'])),
        'collision_rate': results['collisions'] / num_episodes,
        'goal_rate': results['goals'] / num_episodes,
        'timeout_rate': results['timeouts'] / num_episodes,
        'avg_length': float(np.mean(results['lengths'])),
    }
    return summary, results


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Occlusion Robustness Sweep')
    parser.add_argument('--checkpoint', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_path', type=str, default=None)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_split', type=str, default='valid')
    parser.add_argument('--num_episodes', type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument('--output_dir', type=str, default='occlusion_sweep_results')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)

    # Load base config
    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split, num_files=-1)
    _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, args.checkpoint, use_occlusion=None)

    # Determine if checkpoint uses occlusion
    obs_dim_ckpt = _checkpoint_obs_dim(args.checkpoint)
    uses_occlusion = cfg['occupancy_grid'].get('use_occlusion', False)

    print(f"Checkpoint inferred obs_dim: {obs_dim_ckpt}")
    print(f"Config use_occlusion: {uses_occlusion}")

    # Sweep settings
    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]  # pi, pi/2, pi/4, pi/8

    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            print(f"\n{'='*60}")
            print(f"Evaluating view_dist={view_dist}, view_angle={view_angle:.4f}")
            print(f"{'='*60}")

            # Modify config for this run
            run_cfg = cfg.copy()
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_dist'] = view_dist
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_angle'] = view_angle

            try:
                summary, _ = run_eval_for_config(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
                summary['view_dist'] = view_dist
                summary['view_angle'] = view_angle
                all_summaries.append(summary)

                # Save per-run JSON
                fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace('.', '_')
                with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), 'w') as f:
                    json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

                print(f"  Avg Reward: {summary['avg_reward']:.2f}")
                print(f"  Goal Rate: {summary['goal_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Collision Rate: {summary['collision_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Timeout Rate: {summary['timeout_rate']:.2%}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"  ERROR: {e}")

    # Save full summary
    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, 'sweep_summary.json'), 'w') as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"\n{'='*60}")
    print("ROBUSTNESS SWEEP SUMMARY")
    print(f"{'='*60}")
    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Avg Reward':<14} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10} {'Timeout%':<10}")
    print("-" * 80)
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['avg_reward']:<14.2f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%} {s['timeout_rate']:<10.2%}")


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

```python
    visible = self.base_env.scenario.visible_state(
```


So both matter:
- `subscriber.view_dist` / `subscriber.view_angle` affect the base Nocturne observation (ego state, etc.)
- `grid_cfg.forward_dist` / `grid_cfg.lateral_dist` affect the occupancy grid size and the occlusion features.

For a proper robustness sweep, I should probably vary `subscriber.view_dist` and `subscriber.view_angle`, while keeping the grid size constant. The grid is fixed in the network input, so changing grid size would break the agent. But changing `subscriber.view_dist` means the BaseEnv sees fewer objects, which affects the observation returned.

Actually, if `subscriber.view_dist` is reduced, the base env simply sees fewer objects in the `obs_dict`. The occupancy grid logic in Python still reads all objects and stamps them into the grid based on their positions. It doesn't seem to be limited by `subscriber.view_dist`.

Wait, let me look at `_build_observation`:
```python
def _build_observation(self, obs_dict):
    # ...
    visible_set = None
    visible_objects = None
    if self.use_occlusion:
        visible_objects = self._query_visible_objects(ego_veh)
        visible_set = self._match_visible_to_vehicles(ego_veh, visible_objects)
    grid = self._build_occupancy_grid(ego_veh, obs_dict, visible_set)
    # ...
```

And `_build_occupancy_grid` likely uses `obs_dict` which comes from the base env. The base env's `obs_dict` might be affected by `subscriber.view_dist`.

In any case, for the robustness sweep to be meaningful, I should vary *both*:
1. `subscriber.view_dist` (affects what the base env reports)
2. `grid_cfg.forward_dist` and `lateral_dist` (affects the grid generation and occlusion features)

But changing `grid_cfg.forward_dist` changes the scale of the occupancy grid? No, the grid size is fixed: `rows x cols`. The `forward_dist` just maps physical distance to grid indices.

So the most straightforward robustness test for "partial observability" is actually just:
- Change `subscriber.view_dist` (how far the C++ base env sees)
- Change `subscriber.view_angle` (the field of view)

This affects the `obs_dict` that the Python env receives. The occupancy grid builder then stamps whatever is in `obs_dict` into the grid.

But wait, the occupancy grid also has `self.forward_dist` and `self.lateral_dist`. These determine the *extent* of the grid in meters. If I reduce `subscriber.view_dist` but keep grid size the same, the agent simply sees an empty grid beyond the view distance. This is exactly the "sensor degradation" we want to test.

However, if I reduce `subscriber.view_angle` to a very narrow cone (e.g., 0.4 rad), the occupancy grid will show objects only in a narrow wedge. This is also exactly what we want.

So the sweep parameters are:
- `subscriber.view_dist`: [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
- `subscriber.view_angle`: [3.14, 1.57, 0.79, 0.39] (radians)

```python
def _checkpoint_obs_dim(checkpoint_path: str) -> Optional[int]:
    checkpoint = torch.load(checkpoint_path, map_location='cpu', weights_only=False)
    if not isinstance(checkpoint, dict):
        return None
```

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Occlusion Robustness Sweep.

Evaluate a trained checkpoint across varying sensor capabilities
(view distance, view angle) to produce robustness curves for
partially observable driving.
"""

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
from typing import List, Optional, Tuple

import numpy as np
import torch

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.evaluate import _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings, _checkpoint_obs_dim
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config


def run_eval_for_config(checkpoint_path: str, cfg: dict, num_episodes: int = 100) -> dict:
    """Run a single evaluation with a fully prepared config dict."""
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint_path)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0

    results = {
        'rewards': [], 'lengths': [],
        'collisions': 0, 'goals': 0, 'timeouts': 0,
        'collided_list': [], 'goal_list': [], 'timeout_list': [],
        'ttz_vehicles': [], 'ttz_pedestrians': [],
    }

    for ep in range(1, num_episodes + 1):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward = 0.0
        collided = False
        goal = False

        for t in range(cfg['drl']['max_episode_steps']):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            done = terminated or truncated
            total_reward += reward
            collided = collided or info.get('collided', False)
            goal = goal or info.get('goal_achieved', False)
            ttz_vehicle = info.get('ttz_vehicle', 999.0)
            ttz_pedestrian = info.get('ttz_pedestrian', 999.0)
            results['ttz_vehicles'].append(ttz_vehicle)
            results['ttz_pedestrians'].append(ttz_pedestrian)
            if done:
                break

        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results['rewards'].append(total_reward)
        results['lengths'].append(t + 1)
        results['collisions'] += int(collided)
        results['goals'] += int(goal)
        results['timeouts'] += int(timeout)
        results['collided_list'].append(collided)
        results['goal_list'].append(goal)
        results['timeout_list'].append(timeout)

    summary = {
        'num_episodes': num_episodes,
        'avg_reward': float(np.mean(results['rewards'])),
        'std_reward': float(np.std(results['rewards'])),
        'collision_rate': results['collisions'] / num_episodes,
        'goal_rate': results['goals'] / num_episodes,
        'timeout_rate': results['timeouts'] / num_episodes,
        'avg_length': float(np.mean(results['lengths'])),
    }
    return summary, results


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Occlusion Robustness Sweep')
    parser.add_argument('--checkpoint', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_path', type=str, default=None)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_split', type=str, default='valid')
    parser.add_argument('--num_episodes', type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument('--output_dir', type=str, default='occlusion_sweep_results')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)

    # Load base config
    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split, num_files=-1)
    _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, args.checkpoint, use_occlusion=None)

    # Determine if checkpoint uses occlusion
    obs_dim_ckpt = _checkpoint_obs_dim(args.checkpoint)
    uses_occlusion = cfg['occupancy_grid'].get('use_occlusion', False)

    print(f"Checkpoint inferred obs_dim: {obs_dim_ckpt}")
    print(f"Config use_occlusion: {uses_occlusion}")

    # Sweep settings
    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]  # pi, pi/2, pi/4, pi/8

    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            print(f"\n{'='*60}")
            print(f"Evaluating view_dist={view_dist}, view_angle={view_angle:.4f}")
            print(f"{'='*60}")

            # Modify config for this run
            run_cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_dist'] = view_dist
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_angle'] = view_angle

            try:
                summary, _ = run_eval_for_config(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
                summary['view_dist'] = view_dist
                summary['view_angle'] = view_angle
                all_summaries.append(summary)

                # Save per-run JSON
                fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace('.', '_')
                with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), 'w') as f:
                    json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

                print(f"  Avg Reward: {summary['avg_reward']:.2f}")
                print(f"  Goal Rate: {summary['goal_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Collision Rate: {summary['collision_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Timeout Rate: {summary['timeout_rate']:.2%}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"  ERROR: {e}")

    # Save full summary
    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, 'sweep_summary.json'), 'w') as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"\n{'='*60}")
    print("ROBUSTNESS SWEEP SUMMARY")
    print(f"{'='*60}")
    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Avg Reward':<14} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10} {'Timeout%':<10}")
    print("-" * 80)
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['avg_reward']:<14.2f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%} {s['timeout_rate']:<10.2%}")


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Occlusion Robustness Sweep.

Evaluate a trained checkpoint across varying sensor capabilities
(view distance, view angle) to produce robustness curves for
partially observable driving.
"""

import argparse
import copy
import json
import os
import sys

import numpy as np

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.evaluate import _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config


def run_eval_for_config(checkpoint_path: str, cfg: dict, num_episodes: int = 100) -> dict:
    """Run a single evaluation with a fully prepared config dict."""
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint_path)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0

    results = {
        'rewards': [], 'lengths': [],
        'collisions': 0, 'goals': 0, 'timeouts': 0,
        'collided_list': [], 'goal_list': [], 'timeout_list': [],
        'ttz_vehicles': [], 'ttz_pedestrians': [],
    }

    for ep in range(1, num_episodes + 1):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward = 0.0
        collided = False
        goal = False

        for t in range(cfg['drl']['max_episode_steps']):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            done = terminated or truncated
            total_reward += reward
            collided = collided or info.get('collided', False)
            goal = goal or info.get('goal_achieved', False)
            ttz_vehicle = info.get('ttz_vehicle', 999.0)
            ttz_pedestrian = info.get('ttz_pedestrian', 999.0)
            results['ttz_vehicles'].append(ttz_vehicle)
            results['ttz_pedestrians'].append(ttz_pedestrian)
            if done:
                break

        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results['rewards'].append(total_reward)
        results['lengths'].append(t + 1)
        results['collisions'] += int(collided)
        results['goals'] += int(goal)
        results['timeouts'] += int(timeout)
        results['collided_list'].append(collided)
        results['goal_list'].append(goal)
        results['timeout_list'].append(timeout)

    summary = {
        'num_episodes': num_episodes,
        'avg_reward': float(np.mean(results['rewards'])),
        'std_reward': float(np.std(results['rewards'])),
        'collision_rate': results['collisions'] / num_episodes,
        'goal_rate': results['goals'] / num_episodes,
        'timeout_rate': results['timeouts'] / num_episodes,
        'avg_length': float(np.mean(results['lengths'])),
    }
    return summary, results


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Occlusion Robustness Sweep')
    parser.add_argument('--checkpoint', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_path', type=str, default=None)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_split', type=str, default='valid')
    parser.add_argument('--num_episodes', type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument('--output_dir', type=str, default='occlusion_sweep_results')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)

    # Load base config
    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split, num_files=-1)
    _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, args.checkpoint, use_occlusion=None)

    # Sweep settings
    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]  # pi, pi/2, pi/4, pi/8

    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            print(f"\n{'='*60}")
            print(f"Evaluating view_dist={view_dist}, view_angle={view_angle:.4f}")
            print(f"{'='*60}")

            run_cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_dist'] = view_dist
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_angle'] = view_angle

            try:
                summary, _ = run_eval_for_config(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
                summary['view_dist'] = view_dist
                summary['view_angle'] = view_angle
                all_summaries.append(summary)

                fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace('.', '_')
                with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), 'w') as f:
                    json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

                print(f"  Avg Reward: {summary['avg_reward']:.2f}")
                print(f"  Goal Rate: {summary['goal_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Collision Rate: {summary['collision_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Timeout Rate: {summary['timeout_rate']:.2%}")

            except Exception as e:
                print(f"  ERROR: {e}")

    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, 'sweep_summary.json'), 'w') as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"\n{'='*60}")
    print("ROBUSTNESS SWEEP SUMMARY")
    print(f"{'='*60}")
    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Avg Reward':<14} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10} {'Timeout%':<10}")
    print("-" * 80)
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['avg_reward']:<14.2f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%} {s['timeout_rate']:<10.2%}")


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

Run it like this:


This gives you a **robustness curve** showing how collision rate and goal rate degrade as you limit the sensor range/angle. This is your "Partial Observability" and "Robust Occlusion Avoidance" story.

## Phase 3: Add Risk-Aware / Safety-Critical Baselines (Next 1-2 Weeks)

To sharpen novelty, you cannot just show that DDQN works. You need to show that a **risk-aware** version is better or more robust than the baseline.

The repo already has the scaffolding for this in `algos/ppo/r_mappo/`. But to keep your current DDQN path, the simplest comparison is:

### Baseline A: Standard DDQN (Your Current Run)
### Baseline B: DDQN with Risk-Penalized Reward
Modify `collision_avoidance_env.py` in `_compute_reward` to add an extra term:

```python
# Add inside _compute_reward
min_ttz = min(self._ttz_vehicle, self._ttz_pedestrian)
if min_ttz < 3.0 and min_ttz > 0:
    reward -= self.risk_penalty_scale * (3.0 - min_ttz)
```

This explicitly punishes the agent for being in high-risk states even if no collision happened. Run a separate training job with this.

### Baseline C: Behavioral Cloning
The repo has `train_bc.py`. Run it on the same scenarios and compare goal/collision rates.

---

## Phase 4: Statistical Rigor (Run This in Parallel)

You need **multiple seeds** for any claim to be valid.

1. **Finish your current training** (or stop at a good checkpoint).
2. **Launch 3 more independent training runs** with different `--seed` values.
3. **Evaluate all 4 checkpoints** using the same `occlusion_robustness_sweep.py`.
4. **Run:**
   ```bash
   python scripts/compute_statistics.py eval_seed1.json eval_seed2.json eval_seed3.json eval_seed4.json
   ```
   This gives you `mean ± std` and the Mann-Whitney U-test p-values you need for a paper.

---

- `scripts/interpretability/occlusion_behavior.py` — use this to prove your agent slows down with high occlusion.
- `visualize.py` — use this to generate qualitative videos for the paper.

**Missing:** You should add a script to compute saliency or attention over the occupancy grid. This shows *which* parts of the road the agent is looking at. If you have a CNN-based Q-network, you can add a forward hook.

## Assistant

### What Needs to Happen

1. **Fix the sweep**: Keep `use_occlusion: false` and only degrade `subscriber.view_dist` and `subscriber.view_angle`. This tests raw sensor degradation without changing the observation dimension.

Here is the corrected sweep that works with your current checkpoint:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Occlusion Robustness Sweep (Fixed for use_occlusion=False).

Evaluates a trained checkpoint under varying sensor degradation
(view_dist, view_angle) WITHOUT changing the observation dimension.
"""
import argparse
import copy
import json
import os
import sys

import numpy as np

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.evaluate import _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config


def run_eval(checkpoint_path: str, cfg: dict, num_episodes: int = 100):
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint_path)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0

    results = {'rewards': [], 'lengths': [],
               'collisions': 0, 'goals': 0, 'timeouts': 0,
               'collided_list': [], 'goal_list': [], 'timeout_list': []}

    for ep in range(1, num_episodes + 1):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward, collided, goal = 0.0, False, False
        for t in range(cfg['drl']['max_episode_steps']):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            collided = collided or info.get('collided', False)
            goal = goal or info.get('goal_achieved', False)
            total_reward += reward
            if terminated or truncated:
                break
        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results['rewards'].append(total_reward)
        results['lengths'].append(t + 1)
        results['collisions'] += int(collided)
        results['goals'] += int(goal)
        results['timeouts'] += int(timeout)
        results['collided_list'].append(collided)
        results['goal_list'].append(goal)
        results['timeout_list'].append(timeout)

    return {
        'num_episodes': num_episodes,
        'avg_reward': float(np.mean(results['rewards'])),
        'std_reward': float(np.std(results['rewards'])),
        'collision_rate': results['collisions'] / num_episodes,
        'goal_rate': results['goals'] / num_episodes,
        'timeout_rate': results['timeouts'] / num_episodes,
    }


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument('--checkpoint', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_path', type=str, default=None)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_split', type=str, default='valid')
    parser.add_argument('--num_episodes', type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument('--output_dir', type=str, default='occlusion_sweep_results')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)

    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split, num_files=-1)
    _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, args.checkpoint, use_occlusion=False)  # force false

    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]
    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            print(f"\n{'='*60}")
            print(f"Evaluating view_dist={view_dist}, view_angle={view_angle:.4f}")
            print(f"{'='*60}")

            run_cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_dist'] = view_dist
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_angle'] = view_angle

            try:
                summary = run_eval(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
                summary['view_dist'] = view_dist
                summary['view_angle'] = view_angle
                all_summaries.append(summary)

                fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace('.', '_')
                with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), 'w') as f:
                    json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

                print(f"  Goal Rate: {summary['goal_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Collision Rate: {summary['collision_rate']:.2%}")
                print(f"  Timeout Rate: {summary['timeout_rate']:.2%}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"  ERROR: {e}")

    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, 'sweep_summary.json'), 'w') as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"\n{'='*60}")
    print("ROBUSTNESS SWEEP SUMMARY")
    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Avg Reward':<14} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10} {'Timeout%':<10}")
    print("-" * 80)
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['avg_reward']:<14.2f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%} {s['timeout_rate']:<10.2%}")


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

Now, for the **second training run** (the one you need for the paper), launch this alongside your current job:

```bash
  --checkpoint <BASELINE_FINAL.pth> \
  --output_dir sweep_baseline

# Occlusion-aware (trained with occlusion)
python -m examples.drl_collision_avoidance.sweep \
  --checkpoint <OCCLUSION_FINAL.pth> \
  --output_dir sweep_occlusion
```

That’s it. The two sweep JSON folders are your core results for the paper.

how is this

## Assistant

   These are solid results for a driving agent. 95.3% goal rate and 5.1% collision rate on a valid set of 1500 episodes indicates the policy has converged well and is generally safe.

However, some metrics look suspicious:
- **ADE = 956.27 ± 1307.41 m**: This is extremely high and physically impossible for an 80-step (20.6s average length) episode. This strongly suggests a bug in the ADE calculation or a unit/ coordinate mismatch in the expert position query. The high std (1307) confirms outliers/spikey values.
- **FDE = 9.76 ± 8.59 m**: This is much more reasonable, but the contrast with ADE is weird. If ADE is averaging displacement over all timesteps and getting 956m, something is wrong with the expert trajectory fetch or the coordinate system.
- **Jerk = 10.27 m/s³**: For a 0.1s dt, this implies very aggressive accelerations. Looking at the action table ([-1, 0, 2] throttle, [-0.7, -0.2, 0, 0.2, 0.7] steer), the agent can output high accelerations. Whether 10.27 is "good" depends on the paper's baseline.
- **Smoothness = 10.14 rad/s**: Also quite high, suggesting very sharp steering changes.

What does this mean for you?
1. **The high ADE is almost certainly a metric bug**, not an agent bug. Since the agent is achieving 95% goals, it isn't driving 956 meters off course. You should inspect `/tmp/eval_run/eval_baseline.json` to see the per-episode ADE values.
2. The collision rate is low (5.1%), which is excellent for a validation set.
3. The goal rate is very high (95.3%).

For your paper, these numbers show that the agent is functional and safe. You should:
- Check if ADE is a bug in `evaluate.py` (likely the expert position is in a different coordinate frame).
- Compare these numbers against a baseline (like the BC agent or a random agent).
- Run the occlusion training and compare.

First, create the sweep script:

```bash
cat > ~/nocturne/examples/drl_collision_avoidance/sweep.py << 'EOF'
import argparse, copy, json, os, sys
import numpy as np
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config

def run_eval(checkpoint, cfg, num_episodes):
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0

    results = {'rewards': [], 'lengths': [], 'collisions': 0, 'goals': 0, 'timeouts': 0}
    for _ in range(num_episodes):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward, collided, goal = 0.0, False, False
        for t in range(cfg['drl']['max_episode_steps']):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            collided = collided or info.get('collided', False)
            goal = goal or info.get('goal_achieved', False)
            total_reward += reward
            if terminated or truncated:
                break
        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results['rewards'].append(total_reward)
        results['lengths'].append(t + 1)
        results['collisions'] += int(collided)
        results['goals'] += int(goal)
        results['timeouts'] += int(timeout)

    return {
        'num_episodes': num_episodes,
        'avg_reward': float(np.mean(results['rewards'])),
        'collision_rate': results['collisions'] / num_episodes,
        'goal_rate': results['goals'] / num_episodes,
        'timeout_rate': results['timeouts'] / num_episodes,
        'avg_length': float(np.mean(results['lengths'])),
    }

def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument('--checkpoint', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_path', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_split', type=str, default='valid')
    parser.add_argument('--num_episodes', type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument('--output_dir', type=str, default='sweep_results')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split)
    # force use_occlusion inference from checkpoint dimension in a real script

    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]
    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            run_cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_dist'] = view_dist
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_angle'] = view_angle
            summary = run_eval(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
            summary['view_dist'] = view_dist
            summary['view_angle'] = view_angle
            all_summaries.append(summary)

            fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace('.', '_')
            with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), 'w') as f:
                json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, 'summary.json'), 'w') as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10}")
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%}")

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
EOF
```

```bash
  --scenario_path /home/ubuntu/nocturne/tests \
  --num_episodes 200 \
  --output_dir /tmp/sweep_occlusion
```

## Assistant

   The error is a classic dimension mismatch. The checkpoint has `obs_dim = 269`, but the current model has `obs_dim = 265`.

Looking at the `CollisionAvoidanceEnv`:
- Base dimension: `grid_size + 4 + 2 + 3 = grid_size + 9`
- With occlusion: `grid_size + 13`

So:
- `grid_size + 9 = 265` => `grid_size = 256`
- `grid_size + 13 = 269` => `grid_size = 256`

Both have the same `grid_size`, which makes sense. The baseline checkpoint was trained with `use_occlusion=false` (obs_dim = 265), while the occlusion checkpoint was trained with `use_occlusion=true` (obs_dim = 269).

**Step 2: Fix the sweep script so it infers occlusion from the checkpoint**

Replace your current `sweep.py` with this updated version:

```bash
cat > ~/nocturne/examples/drl_collision_avoidance/sweep.py << 'EOF'
import argparse, copy, json, os, sys
import numpy as np
import torch
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.evaluate import _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings, _checkpoint_obs_dim
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config


def run_eval(checkpoint, cfg, num_episodes):
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0

    results = {'rewards': [], 'lengths': [], 'collisions': 0, 'goals': 0, 'timeouts': 0}
    for _ in range(num_episodes):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward, collided, goal = 0.0, False, False
        for t in range(cfg['drl']['max_episode_steps']):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            collided = collided or info.get('collided', False)
            goal = goal or info.get('goal_achieved', False)
            total_reward += reward
            if terminated or truncated:
                break
        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results['rewards'].append(total_reward)
        results['lengths'].append(t + 1)
        results['collisions'] += int(collided)
        results['goals'] += int(goal)
        results['timeouts'] += int(timeout)

    return {
        'num_episodes': num_episodes,
        'avg_reward': float(np.mean(results['rewards'])),
        'collision_rate': results['collisions'] / num_episodes,
        'goal_rate': results['goals'] / num_episodes,
        'timeout_rate': results['timeouts'] / num_episodes,
    }


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument('--checkpoint', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_path', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_split', type=str, default='valid')
    parser.add_argument('--num_episodes', type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument('--output_dir', type=str, default='sweep_results')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split)
    _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, args.checkpoint, use_occlusion=None)

    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]
    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            run_cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_dist'] = view_dist
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_angle'] = view_angle
            summary = run_eval(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
            summary['view_dist'] = view_dist
            summary['view_angle'] = view_angle
            all_summaries.append(summary)

            fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace('.', '_')
            with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), 'w') as f:
                json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, 'summary.json'), 'w') as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10}")
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%}")


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
EOF
```

```python
def _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings(cfg, checkpoint_path, use_occlusion):
    grid_cfg = cfg['occupancy_grid']
    if use_occlusion is not None:
        grid_cfg['use_occlusion'] = bool(use_occlusion)
        return

    ckpt_obs_dim = _checkpoint_obs_dim(checkpoint_path)
    ...
```

Wait, this function checks `ckpt_obs_dim`. If the checkpoint is missing `obs_dim` key (some older DDQN versions don't save it), it falls through to infer from layer shapes. But whatever the path, if `ckpt_obs_dim == occ_dim`, it sets `grid_cfg['use_occlusion'] = True`.

Looking at `ddqn_agent.py` saving code, it might not save `obs_dim` as a top-level key. It might only save the `online_net` state dict and some other metadata.

Wait, the `_checkpoint_obs_dim` function in `evaluate.py` tries `checkpoint.get('obs_dim')`. If that is `None`, it tries to infer from the layer shapes. If neither works, it returns `None`. If it returns `None`, then `_apply_checkpoint_observation_settings` does nothing and leaves `use_occlusion` as it was in the config (which is `false`).

So the issue is that your checkpoint might not have an `obs_dim` field saved, and the layer shape inference might be failing for some reason (maybe because of `noisy` layers or `muon` optimizer, where the weight names don't match the expected `advantage_head.initial_layer.0.weight`).

The root issue in `sweep.py` is that `_apply_checkpoint_observation_settings` doesn't work for their checkpoint format. The checkpoint might not have the `obs_dim` field saved properly.

Looking at the `_checkpoint_obs_dim` function:
```python
def _checkpoint_obs_dim(checkpoint_path: str) -> Optional[int]:
    checkpoint = torch.load(checkpoint_path, map_location='cpu', weights_only=False)
    ...
    if checkpoint.get('obs_dim') is not None:
        return int(checkpoint['obs_dim'])
```

If the checkpoint has `obs_dim`, it returns it. Otherwise, it tries to infer by looking at the layer weights.

If the checkpoint doesn't have `obs_dim` (which is common for older DDQN checkpoints that only save the model state dict), the inference code checks for specific layer names like `advantage_head.initial_layer.0.weight`. If the layer names are different (e.g., due to `noisy` layers or `muon` using different names), it fails.

So the fix for `sweep.py` is simple: since the user knows which checkpoint is which, just pass the `use_occlusion` flag explicitly to `load_config` or modify the script to handle it.

Actually, the simplest fix is to modify `sweep.py` to accept a `--use_occlusion` argument, or simply set it in the config based on the checkpoint.

But the user doesn't need to understand all this. They just want it to work. I will provide a completely fixed `sweep.py` that takes a `--use_occlusion` flag. But actually, that's annoying because they need to remember it.

A better approachPenalty: modify `sweep.py` to correctly detect the dimension.

Actually, the user can just use the Python one-liner I gave them to check `obs_dim`, and then manually set it in the script.

The issue is that `cfg['occupancy_grid']` might be a Hydra `DictConfig`, which `deepcopy` handles differently. If `load_config` returns a `DictConfig`, copying it might not work as expected.

**The Problem:** `sweep.py` is not telling the environment that the checkpoint is occlusion-aware. It defaults to `use_occlusion=false`, making the `obs_dim` `265`, while your checkpoints have `269`.

**Step 1: Get your only baseline back** (if it's still in `/tmp`)

```bash

```

If that file exists, copy it to a safe location:


**Step 2: Fix `sweep.py` so it correctly handles both checkpoint types**

```bash
cat > ~/nocturne/examples/drl_collision_avoidance/sweep.py << 'EOF'
import argparse, copy, json, os, sys
import numpy as np
import torch
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..', '..'))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.evaluate import _checkpoint_obs_dim, _apply_checkpoint_observation_settings
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config


def _infer_use_occlusion(cfg, checkpoint_path):
    """Infer if checkpoint uses occlusion features by checking its obs_dim."""
    ckpt_obs_dim = _checkpoint_obs_dim(checkpoint_path)
    if ckpt_obs_dim is not None:
        grid_size = 3 * int(cfg['occupancy_grid']['rows']) * int(cfg['occupancy_grid']['cols'])
        no_occ_dim = grid_size + 4 + 2 + 3  # 9
        occ_dim = no_occ_dim + 4  # 13
        if ckpt_obs_dim == occ_dim:
            return True
        if ckpt_obs_dim == no_occ_dim:
            return False
    return None


def run_eval(checkpoint, cfg, num_episodes):
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0

    results = {'rewards': [], 'lengths': [], 'collisions': 0, 'goals': 0, 'timeouts': 0}
    for _ in range(num_episodes):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward, collided, goal = 0.0, False, False
        for t in range(cfg['drl']['max_episode_steps']):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            collided = collided or info.get('collided', False)
            goal = goal or info.get('goal_achieved', False)
            total_reward += reward
            if terminated or truncated:
                break
        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results['rewards'].append(total_reward)
        results['lengths'].append(t + 1)
        results['collisions'] += int(collided)
        results['goals'] += int(goal)
        results['timeouts'] += int(timeout)

    return {
        'num_episodes': num_episodes,
        'avg_reward': float(np.mean(results['rewards'])),
        'collision_rate': results['collisions'] / num_episodes,
        'goal_rate': results['goals'] / num_episodes,
        'timeout_rate': results['timeouts'] / num_episodes,
    }


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument('--checkpoint', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_path', type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--scenario_split', type=str, default='valid')
    parser.add_argument('--num_episodes', type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument('--output_dir', type=str, default='sweep_results')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split)

    # Infer and apply occlusion setting
    inferred = _infer_use_occlusion(cfg, args.checkpoint)
    if inferred is not None:
        cfg['occupancy_grid']['use_occlusion'] = inferred
        print(f"Inferred use_occlusion={inferred} from checkpoint")
    else:
        print("WARNING: Could not infer use_occlusion from checkpoint, using config default")

    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]
    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            run_cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_dist'] = view_dist
            run_cfg['subscriber']['view_angle'] = view_angle
            summary = run_eval(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
            summary['view_dist'] = view_dist
            summary['view_angle'] = view_angle
            all_summaries.append(summary)

            fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace('.', '_')
            with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), 'w') as f:
                json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, 'summary.json'), 'w') as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10}")
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%}")


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
EOF
```

**Step 3: Run the sweeps**

If `/tmp/eval_run/ddqn_ep400000.pth` is your baseline (check with `ls -lh` first):

```bash

# Baseline sweep
python3 -m examples.drl_collision_avoidance.sweep \
  --checkpoint /tmp/eval_run/ddqn_ep400000.pth \
  --scenario_path /home/ubuntu/nocturne/tests \
  --num_episodes 200 \
  --output_dir /tmp/sweep_baseline

# Occlusion sweep
python3 -m examples.drl_collision_avoidance.sweep \
  --checkpoint ~/nocturne/checkpoints/drl_collision_avoidance/ddqn_final.pth \
  --scenario_path /home/ubuntu/nocturne/tests \
  --num_episodes 200 \
  --output_dir /tmp/sweep_occlusion
```

If `/tmp/eval_run/ddqn_ep400000.pth` is gone, your baseline is lost and the first command will fail. In that case, skip it and only run the occlusion sweep. If you need a baseline for the paper, you will have to retrain it.

**Step 2: Write a minimal, clean `sweep.py` using `python3` (avoids heredoc truncation)**

```bash
python3 << 'PYEOF'
script = '''
import argparse, copy, json, os, sys
import numpy as np
import torch
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", ".."))

from cfgs.config import set_display_window
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.agent_utils import build_cpu_agent
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.collision_avoidance_env import CollisionAvoidanceEnv
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.evaluate import _checkpoint_obs_dim
from examples.drl_collision_avoidance.scenario_utils import load_config

def run_eval(checkpoint, cfg, num_episodes):
    set_display_window()
    env = CollisionAvoidanceEnv(cfg)
    obs_dim = env.observation_space.shape[0]
    n_actions = env.action_space.n
    agent = build_cpu_agent(cfg, obs_dim, n_actions)
    agent.load(checkpoint)
    agent.epsilon = 0.0
    results = {"rewards": [], "lengths": [], "collisions": 0, "goals": 0, "timeouts": 0}
    for _ in range(num_episodes):
        state, _ = env.reset()
        total_reward, collided, goal = 0.0, False, False
        for t in range(cfg["drl"]["max_episode_steps"]):
            action = agent.select_action(state)
            state, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
            collided = collided or info.get("collided", False)
            goal = goal or info.get("goal_achieved", False)
            total_reward += reward
            if terminated or truncated:
                break
        timeout = not collided and not goal
        results["rewards"].append(total_reward)
        results["lengths"].append(t + 1)
        results["collisions"] += int(collided)
        results["goals"] += int(goal)
        results["timeouts"] += int(timeout)
    return {
        "num_episodes": num_episodes,
        "avg_reward": float(np.mean(results["rewards"])),
        "collision_rate": results["collisions"] / num_episodes,
        "goal_rate": results["goals"] / num_episodes,
        "timeout_rate": results["timeouts"] / num_episodes,
    }

def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--checkpoint", type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--scenario_path", type=str, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--scenario_split", type=str, default="valid")
    parser.add_argument("--num_episodes", type=int, default=100)
    parser.add_argument("--output_dir", type=str, default="sweep_results")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    cfg = load_config(scenario_path=args.scenario_path, scenario_split=args.scenario_split)
    
    # Force use_occlusion=True since only occlusion checkpoint exists
    cfg["occupancy_grid"]["use_occlusion"] = True
    print("Set use_occlusion=True")

    view_dists = [80, 50, 30, 20, 10]
    view_angles = [3.14159, 1.57080, 0.78540, 0.39270]
    all_summaries = []

    for view_dist in view_dists:
        for view_angle in view_angles:
            run_cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
            run_cfg["subscriber"]["view_dist"] = view_dist
            run_cfg["subscriber"]["view_angle"] = view_angle
            summary = run_eval(args.checkpoint, run_cfg, args.num_episodes)
            summary["view_dist"] = view_dist
            summary["view_angle"] = view_angle
            all_summaries.append(summary)
            fname = f"vd{view_dist}_va{view_angle:.4f}.json".replace(".", "_")
            with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, fname), "w") as f:
                json.dump(summary, f, indent=2)

    with open(os.path.join(args.output_dir, "summary.json"), "w") as f:
        json.dump(all_summaries, f, indent=2)

    print(f"{'View Dist':<12} {'View Angle':<12} {'Goal%':<10} {'Coll%':<10}")
    for s in all_summaries:
        print(f"{s['view_dist']:<12} {s['view_angle']:<12.4f} {s['goal_rate']:<10.2%} {s['collision_rate']:<10.2%}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
'''
path = os.path.expanduser("~/nocturne/examples/drl_collision_avoidance/sweep.py")
with open(path, "w") as f:
    f.write(script)
print(f"Wrote {path}")
PYEOF
```

```bash
  --scenario_path /home/ubuntu/nocturne/tests \

  --output_dir /tmp/sweep_occlusion
```

I will keep the answer concise.  It's slow because it's running 20 combinations × 200 episodes = **4,000 total eval episodes** on a single CPU.

Here's the math:
- 5 `view_dist` values × 4 `view_angle` values = 20 sensor configurations.
- 200 episodes per configuration = 4,000 full scenario rollouts.
- Each rollout is ~20 steps, so roughly **80,000 env steps** total.

At CPU eval speed, this can take **30–90 minutes**. The "frame stacking" warnings are just printed once per new `CollisionAvoidanceEnv` creation (once per config combo); they don't affect speed.

**Run it in `tmux` so it doesn't die if your SSH disconnects:**

```bash
tmux new -s sweep_run
cd ~/nocturne

python3 -m examples.drl_collision_avoidance.sweep \
  --checkpoint ~/nocturne/checkpoints/drl_collision_avoidance/ddqn_final.pth \

  --num_episodes 200 \
```

Then detach with `Ctrl+B, D`.

While it runs, you can attach and check progress:
```bash
tmux attach -t sweep_run
```

If you want to speed it up, you can reduce `--num_episodes` to `100` per config. That's still 2,000 episodes, which is enough for decent statistics.

## User

i am stopping the ec2 right now, anyway the data is on ebs, it should perstain

## Assistant

    Good. If the data is on EBS and the instance is just stopped (not terminated), the EBS volume persists and the data will be there when they start the instance again.

However, if the process was killed by the stop, the sweep will be interrupted. They need to know:
1. Data on EBS persists across stops.
2. To resume, they just start the instance again and re-attach the volume (if it's not the root volume, but for a standard EC2 stop/start, the root EBS volume persists).
3. If the sweep was running, it will need to be restarted (unless they used `tmux` or `screen` and the process can survive the stop... which it can't. A stop is like a power-off. `tmux` sessions do not survive a stop).

So the advice is:
- Data is safe.
- The process is dead.
- When they start the EC2 again, they need to re-run the sweep.
- If they want to run it without staying connected, they should use `tmux` and detach before stopping, but since they are stopping now, it's too late for the current run.

Also, they should make sure to sync the partial results to S3 before stopping, just in case.  Your data is safe if the EC2 is just **stopped** (not terminated). EBS volumes persist across stops. When you start it again, everything in `~/nocturne/`, `~/checkpoints/`, and `/tmp/` will still be there.

But if the sweep was still running, it got killed by the stop. `tmux` doesn't survive a power-off.

**Before you stop, quickly sync any partial results to S3:**

```bash
aws s3 sync /tmp/sweep_occlusion s3://btp-rl-data-mumbai/nocturne_training/sweep_occlusion_partial/
```

**When you start the EC2 again:**
1. Start the instance.
2. SSH back in.
3. Re-run the sweep in a `tmux` session so it survives disconnects.

```bash
tmux new -s sweep_run
cd ~/nocturne

# Re-run the occlusion sweep

  --checkpoint ~/nocturne/checkpoints/drl_collision_avoidance/ddqn_final.pth \
  --scenario_path /home/ubuntu/nocturne/tests \
  --num_episodes 200 \
  --output_dir /tmp/sweep_occlusion

# Detach with Ctrl+B, D
```

If you already stopped the instance without syncing, just start it again and re-run. The checkpoint and code are still on the EBS volume.
